The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 6?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>-0.3</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>0.9</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/7 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.29</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>1.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>9</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 1?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)/5 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/7 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.57</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup>/5 =</span> <strong>0.8</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(7&minus;5)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;15)/15 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/15 =</span> <strong>15</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;15)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>37.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;15)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>225</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 8.00. The other faces contribute 8.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.00&minus;8.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(8.00+8.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>2.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.00+0 =</span> <strong>8</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.00+8.00 =</span> <strong>16</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 4 is 0.33. The other faces contribute 0.50 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.33+0.50)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.14</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50&minus;0.33 =</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.50+0 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.33+0.50 =</span> <strong>0.83</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 5?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;20)/20 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/20 =</span> <strong>20</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;20)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>66.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;20)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>400</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 3?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6&minus;8)/8 =</span> <strong>-0.25</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/8 =</span> <strong>0.5</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6&minus;8)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(6&minus;8)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 0.80. The other faces contribute 10.80 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.80+10.80)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.93</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80&minus;0.80 =</span> <strong>10</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>10.80+0 =</span> <strong>10.8</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.80+10.80 =</span> <strong>11.6</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 20.00. The other faces contribute 20.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00&minus;20.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20.00+20.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>6.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00+0 =</span> <strong>20</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00+20.00 =</span> <strong>40</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 1 is 0.40. The other faces contribute 0.60 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.40+0.60)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.60&minus;0.40 =</span> <strong>0.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.60+0 =</span> <strong>0.6</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.40+0.60 =</span> <strong>1</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 20.00. The other faces contribute 20.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00&minus;20.00 =</span> <strong>0</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(20.00+20.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>6.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00+0 =</span> <strong>20</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>20.00+20.00 =</span> <strong>40</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 6 is 1.50. The other faces contribute 8.17 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(1.50+8.17)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.61</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.17&minus;1.50 =</span> <strong>6.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>8.17+0 =</span> <strong>8.17</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>1.50+8.17 =</span> <strong>9.67</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 6?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;6)/6 =</span> <strong>-1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;6)<sup>2</sup>/6 =</span> <strong>6</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0&minus;6)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>36</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 2 is 0.80. The other faces contribute 2.10 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.80+2.10)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.48</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.10&minus;0.80 =</span> <strong>1.3</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.10+0 =</span> <strong>2.1</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.80+2.10 =</span> <strong>2.9</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 0.20. The other faces contribute 2.70 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.20+2.70)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.48</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.70&minus;0.20 =</span> <strong>2.5</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>2.70+0 =</span> <strong>2.7</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.20+2.70 =</span> <strong>2.9</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>Which calculation gives the contribution from face 2?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)/10 =</span> <strong>0.2</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/12 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/10 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Correct	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup>/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(12&minus;10)<sup>2</sup> =</span> <strong>4</strong>	Incorrect

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 0.27. The other faces contribute 0.40 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.27+0.40)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>0.11</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.40&minus;0.27 =</span> <strong>0.13</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.40+0 =</span> <strong>0.4</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.27+0.40 =</span> <strong>0.67</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 5 is 0.67. The other faces contribute 9.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.67+9.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.61</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>9.00&minus;0.67 =</span> <strong>8.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>9.00+0 =</span> <strong>9</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.67+9.00 =</span> <strong>9.67</strong>	Correct

The null hypothesis is that each face has probability 1/6 on every roll.</p><p>Each face contributes (observed &minus; expected)<sup>2</sup> / expected to &chi;<sup>2</sup>. The arithmetic is shown in each choice; no calculator is needed.</p><p>The contribution from face 3 is 0.67. The other faces contribute 9.00 in total. Which calculation combines them to give &chi;<sup>2</sup>?</p>	<span style='font-size:85%;'>(0.67+9.00)/6 &asymp;</span> <strong>1.61</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>9.00&minus;0.67 =</span> <strong>8.33</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>9.00+0 =</span> <strong>9</strong>	Incorrect	<span style='font-size:85%;'>0.67+9.00 =</span> <strong>9.67</strong>	Correct
